\begin{resultboxPartiv}[Contribution VI: an operator logarithm]
The infinite sinc product is the Fourier symbol of an infinite product of
uniform averaging operators.  Its logarithm is an even differential operator
whose coefficients are simultaneously sparse cumulants, Bernoulli numbers,
and zeta values.  On polynomials the exponential representation is exact; on
band-limited functions it is an exact multiplier; globally the averaging
product supplies the canonical interpretation.
\end{resultboxPartiv}

\section{Twisted Poisson and zero-slope representations}
\label{p5:sec:Zak}

The critical half-integer cosine series is one member of a continuous family
obtained by twisting Poisson summation.  This family also yields a representation
of $F$ through the derivatives of $\Phi$ at its integer zeros.

\subsection{A one-parameter Zak family}

For real $\alpha$, define
\begin{equation}
 Z_u(x,\alpha)=\sum_{n\in\IntegerNumbers}u(x+n)\EulerE^{-2\pi\ImaginaryUnit n\alpha}.   \label{p5:eq:Zak-def}
\end{equation}
Rapid decay of $\Phi$ and compact support of $u$ justify Poisson summation
absolutely in the forms used below.

\begin{theorem}[Twisted spectral representation of $F$]
\label{p5:thm:twisted-F}
For $0\le x\le1$,
\begin{equation}
 Z_u(x,\alpha)
 =\sum_{k\in\IntegerNumbers}\Phi(k+\alpha)
   \EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit(k+\alpha)x}                                \label{p5:eq:Zak-Poisson}
\end{equation}
and also
\begin{equation}
 Z_u(x,\alpha)=1+(\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit\alpha}-1)F(x).             \label{p5:eq:Zak-fold}
\end{equation}
Hence, whenever $\alpha\notin\IntegerNumbers$,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 F(x)=\frac{
   \sum_{k\in\IntegerNumbers}\Phi(k+\alpha)
       \EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit(k+\alpha)x}-1}
  {\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit\alpha}-1}.}                              \label{p5:eq:F-twisted-Poisson}
\end{equation}
Using polyphase factors, each coefficient may be replaced by
$\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}\Psi_r((k+\alpha)/2^a)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The modulated Poisson formula gives \eqref{p5:eq:Zak-Poisson}.  On the interval
$0<x<1$, only the translates $n=0$ and $n=-1$ meet the support of $u$, so
\[
 Z_u(x,\alpha)=u(x)+\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit\alpha}u(x-1)
 =1-F(x)+\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit\alpha}F(x).
\]
Continuity supplies the endpoint values.
\end{proof}

At $\alpha=1/2$, \eqref{p5:eq:F-twisted-Poisson} becomes the real cosine series
\begin{equation}
 F(x)=\frac12-
 \sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Phi(k+\tfrac12)
   \cos((2k+1)\pi x),                                     \label{p5:eq:F-half-integer-cosine}
\end{equation}
which is the shifted form of \eqref{p5:eq:critical-cosine}.  Rational values of
$\alpha$ give cyclotomic spectral expansions; irrational values give a
continuous family of quasiperiodic representations.

\subsection{Differentiating through the untwisted limit}

Both numerator and denominator of \eqref{p5:eq:F-twisted-Poisson} vanish at
$\alpha=0$.  Differentiation gives a new zero-jet representation.

\begin{corollary}[Integer-zero slope series]
\label{p5:cor:zero-slope-F}
For $0\le x\le1$,
\begin{align}
 F(x)
 &=x+\frac1{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit}
   \sum_{k\in\IntegerNumbers\setminus\{0\}}\Phi'(k)\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit kx}    \label{p5:eq:F-zero-slope-complex}\\
 &=x+\frac1\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Phi'(k)
   \sin(2\pi kx)                                         \label{p5:eq:F-zero-slope-real}\\
 &=x-\frac1\pi\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}
   \frac{\Phi(m+\tfrac12)}{2m+1}
   \sin(2\pi(2m+1)x).                                    \label{p5:eq:F-half-slope-series}
\end{align}
Only odd positive integers contribute in the middle series.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Differentiate \eqref{p5:eq:Zak-Poisson} at $\alpha=0$ and use
\eqref{p5:eq:Zak-fold}.  Since $\Phi(0)=1$, $\Phi'(0)=0$, and
$\Phi(k)=0$ for nonzero integers, division by $2\pi\ImaginaryUnit$ gives
\eqref{p5:eq:F-zero-slope-complex}.  Pair $k$ with $-k$ to obtain the real sine
series.  If $k$ is even, the zero has order at least two, so $\Phi'(k)=0$.
For odd $k=2m+1$, the first sinc factor has a simple zero and
\begin{equation}
 \Phi'(2m+1)=-\frac{\Phi(m+\tfrac12)}{2m+1},               \label{p5:eq:odd-zero-slope}
\end{equation}
which gives \eqref{p5:eq:F-half-slope-series}.
\end{proof}

The transform formula \eqref{p5:eq:F-Fourier} also yields, for real $\xi\ne0$,
\begin{align}
 \int_0^1F(x)\cos(2\pi\xi x)\,\Differential x
 &=\frac{\sin(2\pi\xi)-\Phi(\xi/2)\sin(\pi\xi)}{2\pi\xi}, \label{p5:eq:F-cos-transform}\\
 \int_0^1F(x)\sin(2\pi\xi x)\,\Differential x
 &=\frac{\Phi(\xi/2)\cos(\pi\xi)-\cos(2\pi\xi)}{2\pi\xi}. \label{p5:eq:F-sin-transform}
\end{align}
At integer frequencies the cosine coefficients vanish, while the sine
coefficients are explicit samples of $\Phi$.

\begin{resultboxPartiv}[Contribution VII: a continuous spectral family]
The usual half-integer cosine expansion sits inside a twisted Poisson family
parameterized by $\alpha$.  Letting the twist tend to zero converts spectral
values into spectral slopes and yields a sine series for $F$ supported exactly
on the simple odd zeros of the Rvachev Fourier product.
\end{resultboxPartiv}

\section{Sparse lobe signs and base-\texorpdfstring{$2^r$}{2-to-the-r} digit sums}
\label{p5:sec:sparse-signs}

The staircase zero multiplicities of $\Psi_r$ generate a natural automatic
sign sequence.  Polyphase recombination then factors the ordinary Thue--Morse
sign into $r$ such sparse signs.

Let $s_b(n)$ denote the sum of the base-$b$ digits of $n$.

\begin{theorem}[Lobe signs of a sparse sinc product]
\label{p5:thm:sparse-lobe-sign}
For $n\ge0$ and any $x\in(n,n+1)$,
\begin{align}
 \Sign\Psi_r(x)
 &=(-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_r(j)}                             \label{p5:eq:sparse-sign-zero-count}\\
 &=(-1)^{n+\sum_{\ell\ge1}\Floor{n/2^{r\ell}}}    \label{p5:eq:sparse-sign-floor}\\
 &=\boxed{(-1)^{s_{2^r}(n)}}.                             \label{p5:eq:sparse-sign-base}
\end{align}
Thus the signs of the consecutive real lobes of $\Psi_r$ form the parity-of-
digit-sum sequence in base $2^r$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The sign starts positive on $(0,1)$ and flips once for each zero multiplicity
crossed, giving \eqref{p5:eq:sparse-sign-zero-count}.  Since
$m_r(j)=1+\Floor{\TwoAdicValuation(j)/r}$,
\[
 \sum_{j=1}^{n}\Floor{\frac{\TwoAdicValuation(j)}r}
 =\sum_{\ell\ge1}\#\{j\le n:2^{r\ell}\mid j\}
 =\sum_{\ell\ge1}\Floor{\frac n{2^{r\ell}}}.
\]
For $b=2^r$, the base-$b$ Legendre identity is
\[
 \sum_{\ell\ge1}\Floor{\frac n{b^\ell}}
 =\frac{n-s_b(n)}{b-1}.
\]
Because $b-1$ is odd and $b$ is even,
$n+(n-s_b(n))/(b-1)$ has the same parity as $s_b(n)$.
\end{proof}

At $r=1$, \eqref{p5:eq:sparse-sign-base} is the familiar formula
$\Sign\Phi(x)=\eps_n$ on the $n$th lobe.  For $r>1$ it supplies a companion
automatic sequence attached to the sparse Rvachev density.

\begin{corollary}[Polyphase factorization of the Thue--Morse sign]
\label{p5:cor:TM-base-factorization}
For every $r\ge1$ and $n\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \eps_n
 =\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{s_{2^r}(\Floor{n/2^a})}.} \label{p5:eq:TM-base-factorization}
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \tau(n)\equiv
 \sum_{a=0}^{r-1}s_{2^r}(\Floor{n/2^a})\pmod2.}   \label{p5:eq:TM-base-parity}
\end{equation}
The same representation is analytic:
\begin{equation}
 \eps_n
 =\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}
   \Sign\Psi_r\!\left(\frac{n+1/2}{2^a}\right).           \label{p5:eq:TM-sparse-lobe-product}
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Take signs in the polyphase product
$\Phi(n+1/2)=\prod_a\Psi_r((n+1/2)/2^a)$.  The argument of the $a$th factor
lies in the lobe indexed by $\Floor{n/2^a}$.  Apply
\cref{p5:thm:sparse-lobe-sign} and use
$\Sign\Phi(n+1/2)=\eps_n$.  The unsigned parity form is the exponent modulo two.
\end{proof}

An entirely arithmetical proof follows by summing the floor exponents:
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\sum_{m\ge0}
 \Floor{\frac{n}{2^{a+rm}}}
 =\sum_{j\ge0}\Floor{\frac{n}{2^j}}
 =2n-\BinaryDigitSum(n),                                               \label{p5:eq:floor-polyphase-TM}
\end{equation}
whose parity is $\BinaryDigitSum(n)$.  Formula
\eqref{p5:eq:TM-base-factorization} therefore expresses binary digit parity as a
cyclic product of overlapping base-$2^r$ digit parities.

\begin{corollary}[Thue--Morse from odd zero slopes]
\label{p5:cor:TM-zero-slopes}
For every $n\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \eps_n=-\Sign\Phi'(2n+1),
 \qquad
 \tau(n)=\frac{1+\Sign\Phi'(2n+1)}2.}                      \label{p5:eq:TM-zero-slope-sign}
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Equation \eqref{p5:eq:odd-zero-slope} says
$\Phi'(2n+1)=-\Phi(n+1/2)/(2n+1)$.  The lobe sign of the half-integer value is
$\eps_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{resultboxPartiv}[Contribution VIII: a new digital factorization]
The sparse component does not merely split amplitudes.  Its lobe signs are
base-$2^r$ digit-parity characters, and their $r$ shifted polyphase copies
multiply to the binary Thue--Morse character.  This gives both a new purely
digital formula and a product of analytic sign samples.  Odd zero slopes of the
full Fourier image give a second pointwise analytic representation.
\end{resultboxPartiv}

\section{Representation concordance}
\label{p5:sec:concordance}

This section gathers the formulas into a compact lookup index.  A reader
interested in a particular mode---probability, transform, product, series,
operator, geometry, or combinatorics---can use the rightmost column to locate
the hypotheses and proof.  The list includes both corpus baseline and the new
polyphase layer; it is not a novelty list.

\subsection{Rvachev's up-function and its Fourier image}

\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.22}
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.18\textwidth}p{0.69\textwidth}p{0.07\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Viewpoint & Representation & Status\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Viewpoint & Representation & Status\\
\midrule
\endhead
Random series &
$X=\sum_{n\ge0}2^{-n-1}U_n$, with $u$ the density of $X$. & \statusknown\\
Infinite cube &
$u(x)=\int_{[-1,1]^{\NaturalNumbers}}\delta(x-\sum2^{-n-1}v_n)\prod\Differential v_n/2$ in the distributional sense. & \statusknown\\
Finite convolution &
$u=\lim_{N\to\infty}g_{1,N}$, where $g_{1,N}$ is an explicit nonuniform box spline. & \statusknown\\
Self-CDF &
$u(x)=\Probability(X\le2x+1)$ for $-1\le x\le0$, equivalently $u(x)=F(x+1)$. & \statusknown\\
Delay equation &
$u'(x)=2(u(2x+1)-u(2x-1))$. & \statusknown\\
Derivative cascade &
$u^{(N)}(x)=2^{N(N+1)/2}\sum_{k<2^N}\eps_k
u(2^Nx+2^N-1-2k)$. & \statusknown\\
Fourier inversion &
$u(x)=\int_{\RealNumbers}\Phi(z)\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit zx}\Differential z
=2\int_0^\infty\Phi(z)\cos(2\pi zx)\Differential z$. & \statusknown\\
Characteristic expectation &
$\Phi(z)=\Expectation\EulerE^{-2\pi\ImaginaryUnit zX}$. & \statusknown\\
Sinc product &
$\Phi(z)=\prod_{n\ge0}\SincPi(z/2^n)$. & \statusknown\\
Triangular cosine product &
$\Phi(z)=\prod_{m\ge1}\cos(\pi z/2^m)^m$. & \statusknown\\
Canonical zero product &
$\Phi(z)=\prod_{n\ge1}(1-z^2/n^2)^{1+\TwoAdicValuation(n)}$. & \statusknown\\
Gamma product &
$\Phi(z)=\prod_{n\ge0}[\Gamma(1+z/2^n)\Gamma(1-z/2^n)]^{-1}$. & \statusknown\\
Logarithmic zeta series &
$\log\Phi(z)=-\sum_{m\ge1}\zeta(2m)z^{2m}/[m(1-4^{-m})]$, $|z|<1$. & \statusknown\\
Cotangent logarithmic derivative &
$\Phi'/\Phi=\sum_{j\ge0}[(\pi/2^j)\cot(\pi z/2^j)-1/z]$. & \statusderived\\
Tangent logarithmic derivative &
$\Phi'/\Phi=-\pi\sum_{m\ge1}m2^{-m}\tan(\pi z/2^m)$. & \statusderived\\
Partial fractions &
$\Phi'/\Phi=-2z\sum_{n\ge1}(1+\TwoAdicValuation(n))/(n^2-z^2)$. & \statusknown\\
Moment-generating product &
$M(s)=\prod_{n\ge0}\sinh(s/2^{n+1})/(s/2^{n+1})$. & \statusknown\\
Moment series &
$M(s)=\sum_{m\ge0}\mu_ms^m/m!$, with
$\mu_m=\cB_m(\kappa_1,\ldots,\kappa_m)$. & \statusknown\\
Cumulants &
$\kappa_{2m}=B_{2m}/[2m(1-4^{-m})]$, $\kappa_{2m+1}=0$. & \statusknown\\
Critical cosine series &
$u(x)=\tfrac12+\sum_{k\ge0}\Phi(k+\tfrac12)\cos((2k+1)\pi x)$. & \statusknown\\
Partition of unity &
$\sum_{j\in\IntegerNumbers}u(x-j)=1$. & \statusknown\\
Twisted Zak transform &
$\sum_nu(x+n)\EulerE^{-2\pi\ImaginaryUnit n\alpha}
=\sum_k\Phi(k+\alpha)\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit(k+\alpha)x}$. & \statusderived\\
Legendre expansion &
$u(x)=\sum_{n\ge0}a_{2n}P_{2n}(x)$,
$a_{2n}=\frac{4n+1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}u(x)P_{2n}(x)\Differential x$;
all coefficients reduce to moments. & \statusknown\\
Haar, Walsh, and Schauder &
Exact rational multiresolution expansions and finite Walsh products are developed in \cite{p5:repoMultiresolution}. & \statusknown\\
Cauchy transform &
$R(z)=\int u(x)/(z-x)\Differential x=\sum_{m\ge0}\mu_mz^{-m-1}$. & \statusknown\\
Jacobi fraction &
$R(z)=1/(z-a_0-b_1/(z-a_1-b_2/(\cdots)))$, with coefficients determined by the moment Hankel determinants. & \statusknown\\
Fredholm determinant &
$\Phi(z)=\det(I-z^2T)$ for eigenvalues $n^{-2}$ of multiplicity $1+\TwoAdicValuation(n)$. & \statusknown\\
Spectral zeta &
$\sum_{n\ge1}(1+\TwoAdicValuation(n))n^{-s}=\zeta(s)/(1-2^{-s})$. & \statusknown\\
Heat trace &
$K(t)=\sum_{n\ge1}(1+\TwoAdicValuation(n))\EulerE^{-tn^2}$ and
$\int_0^\infty t^{s/2-1}K(t)\Differential t=\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)/(1-2^{-s})$. & \statusderived\\
Polyphase product &
$\Phi(z)=\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}\Psi_r(z/2^a)$. & \statusnew\\
Polyphase convolution &
$u=*_{a=0}^{r-1}\bigl(2^ag_r(2^a\,\cdot)\bigr)$. & \statusnew\\
Radon section &
$u(t)=\Norm{w_r}^{-1}\int_{w_r\cdot y=t}\prod_ag_r(y_a)\Differential\sigma$. & \statusnew\\
Averaging product &
$u=(\prod_{j\ge0}\cU_{2^{-j-1}})\delta_0$. & \statusnew\\
Differential exponential &
$u=\exp(\sum_{m\ge1}B_{2m}D^{2m}/[2m(2m)!(1-4^{-m})])\delta_0$, interpreted as in \cref{p5:sec:operators}. & \statusnew\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\begin{statusboxPartx}[How to read the concordance]
The atlas is deliberately redundant across mathematical languages.  For
example, the sinc product, random series, averaging operator, cumulant series,
canonical determinant, and Radon section are not six unrelated objects; they
are six realizations of one law.  The value of the concordance is precisely
that a calculation difficult in one realization may become elementary in
another.
\end{statusboxPartx}

\section{Rigidity and extraction of the polyphase factor}
\label{p5:sec:rigidity}

The sparse factor is not an arbitrary choice among many possible entire
factors.  Once all $r$ phases are required to be dyadic dilates of one
normalized entire function, the factor is forced.

\begin{theorem}[Analytic rigidity of the common polyphase factor]
\label{p5:thm:polyphase-rigidity}
Let $H$ be entire with $H(0)=1$ and suppose
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}H(z/2^a)                         \label{p5:eq:H-factor-assumption}
\end{equation}
for all $z\in\ComplexNumbers$.  Then
\begin{equation}
 H(z)=\Psi_r(z).                                           \label{p5:eq:H-equals-Psi}
\end{equation}
In particular, $\Psi_r$ is the unique normalized entire characteristic
function whose first $r$ dyadic dilates convolve to the up-law.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Near zero, choose the analytic logarithms with value zero.  If
$\log H(z)=\sum_{n\ge1}h_nz^n$, then
\[
 \log\Phi(z)=\sum_{n\ge1}h_n
 \left(\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}2^{-an}\right)z^n.
\]
The finite geometric multiplier never vanishes.  Hence every $h_n$ is uniquely
determined by the Taylor series of $\log\Phi$.  Odd coefficients vanish, and
for $n=2m$ one obtains
\[
 h_{2m}=-\frac{\zeta(2m)}{m(1-2^{-2rm})},
\]
the coefficients of \eqref{p5:eq:Psi-log-zeta}.  Thus $H=\Psi_r$ near zero;
the identity theorem extends equality to the plane.
\end{proof}

There is also an explicit extraction product using only the full Fourier image.
Since $\Phi(z)/\Phi(z/2)=\SincPi(z)$,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \Psi_r(z)=\prod_{m=0}^{\infty}
 \frac{\Phi(z/2^{rm})}{\Phi(z/2^{rm+1})}.}                \label{p5:eq:Psi-from-Phi-ratios}
\end{equation}
Apparent singularities in individual ratios at common zeros are removable;
the ratio is globally the entire sinc factor.  Formula
\eqref{p5:eq:Psi-from-Phi-ratios} is a multiplicative scale decimator: it deletes
all Fourier scales except those congruent to zero modulo $r$.

Conversely, the full product is reconstructed by the finite interpolation
\eqref{p5:eq:polyphase-product}.  Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \Phi
 \xrightarrow{\text{scale decimation}}\Psi_r
 \xrightarrow{\text{$r$ dilates and multiplication}}\Phi                 \label{p5:eq:analysis-synthesis-cycle}
\end{equation}
is an exact analysis--synthesis cycle, analogous to a polyphase filter bank but
in the multiplicative algebra of entire characteristic functions.

\begin{resultboxPartiv}[Contribution IX: uniqueness]
The modular-scale component is uniquely determined by the requirement that one
entire factor and its first $r-1$ dyadic dilates multiply to the Rvachev
Fourier image.  It can be extracted from the full image by an infinite product
of adjacent-scale ratios and synthesized back by a finite product.
\end{resultboxPartiv}

\section{Numerical and exact verification}
\label{p5:sec:numerics}

All identities in the main theorem chain are proved symbolically.  The
numerical work serves three narrower purposes: detecting normalization errors,
checking complex-valued product regrouping at high precision, and making the
jump-measure geometry visible.  The file
\texttt{numerical\_experiments.py} is included in the archive and uses only
Python, mpmath, SymPy, NumPy, and Matplotlib.

\subsection{Verification protocol}

The script performs four independent checks.
\begin{enumerate}
\item It evaluates $\Phi(z)$ and
$\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}\Psi_r(z/2^a)$ at real and genuinely complex points for
$r=2,3,4,5$, using 90 decimal digits.
\item It compares the direct finite atomic transform
$\sum_k\eps_k\EulerE^{-2\pi\ImaginaryUnit zx_k}$\linebreak
with $(2\pi\ImaginaryUnit z)^Nq^{\binom N2}\Psi_r(z)/\Psi_r(q^Nz)$.
\item It constructs finite moments from the hyperbolic MGF in exact rational
arithmetic and independently reconstructs them from the signed high power sum
\eqref{p5:eq:moment-from-TM}.
\item It verifies the cumulant recombination
\eqref{p5:eq:cumulant-recombine} symbolically for orders $2$ through $12$ and
$r=2,3,4,5$.
\end{enumerate}

\midrule

Fourier deconvolution quotient & 9 & $3.64\times10^{-90}$\\
Moment/power-sum reconstruction & 18 & exactly $0$\\
Cumulant recombination & 24 & exactly $0$\\
\bottomrule

The jump-comb visualization in \cref{p5:fig:TM-jump-comb} is produced from the
exact subset sums, not by differentiating noisy sampled data.  This matters:
the highest classical derivative is piecewise constant and its jumps are
mathematically exact Dirac masses in the next distributional derivative.

\subsection{Reproduction}

From the extracted archive directory, run
\begin{lstlisting}[style=python]
python numerical_experiments.py
\end{lstlisting}
The script writes
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{polyphase\_product\_checks.csv};
\item \texttt{fourier\_deconvolution\_checks.csv};
\item \texttt{power\_sum\_moment\_checks.csv};
\item \texttt{polyphase\_cumulant\_checks.csv};
\item \texttt{operator\_coefficients.csv};
\item the two PDF/PNG figures used in this report; and
\item \texttt{verification\_log.txt}.
\end{itemize}
No network access, random seed, or machine-dependent numerical integration is
required.

\begin{warning}
A tiny residual is not used as a proof of an infinite-product identity.  The
proofs precede the experiments.  The checks are valuable because the formulas
contain several easily misplaced factors: $2\pi$, the centered support radius,
$q^{\binom N2}$, the derivative sign $(-1)^N$, and the distinction between
$\Psi_r(q^Nz)$ and $\Psi_r(z/2^N)$.
\end{warning}

\section{Conjectures and frontier directions}
\label{p5:sec:frontiers}

The statements in this section are separated sharply from the proved results.
Some are precise conjectures; others are research programs where fixing the
correct normalization is itself part of the work.  All concern structures
created or clarified by the polyphase decomposition.

\subsection{Uniform all-orders deconvolution}

Define coefficients $a_{r,\ell}$ by the formal identity
\begin{equation}
 \exp\!\left(-\sum_{m\ge1}c_{r,m}t^mD^{2m}\right)
 =\sum_{\ell\ge0}a_{r,\ell}t^\ell D^{2\ell}.              \label{p5:eq:ar-ell-def}
\end{equation}
Thus
\begin{equation}
 a_{r,0}=1,
 \qquad a_{r,1}=-c_{r,1},
 \qquad a_{r,2}=-c_{r,2}+\frac{c_{r,1}^2}{2}.             \label{p5:eq:first-ar-ell}
\end{equation}

\begin{conjecture}[Strong spline-tail expansion]
\label{p5:conj:strong-tail}
For fixed $r,m,L\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 \left\|D^m g_{r,N}-
   \sum_{\ell=0}^{L-1}a_{r,\ell}q^{2\ell N}
      D^{m+2\ell}g_r\right\|_{\infty}
 =\BigO_{r,m,L}(q^{2LN})                                  \label{p5:eq:strong-tail-conj}
\end{equation}
for all sufficiently large $N$.
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 q^{-2N}(g_{r,N}^{(m)}-g_r^{(m)})
 \longrightarrow-c_{r,1}g_r^{(m+2)}                       \label{p5:eq:first-tail-limit}
\end{equation}
uniformly.
\end{conjecture}

The local Fourier expansion proves the coefficient prediction.  A proof of the
uniform remainder must use the exact quotient
$\Psi_r(z)/\Psi_r(q^Nz)$ to control the high-frequency region where the formal
reciprocal has poles.  Compact support and superalgebraic Fourier decay make the
conjecture plausible, but the cancellation near the moving zero lattice should
be handled explicitly rather than hidden in a Taylor argument.

\subsection{Nondegeneracy of sparse Mellin harmonics}

After subtracting the elementary local and mean terms, let $P_r$ denote the
periodic fluctuation in the regularized asymptotics of $\log|\Psi_r(t)|$ or
$\log M_r(t)$.  Normalize its period to $r$:
\begin{equation}
 P_r(\theta)=\sum_{k\in\IntegerNumbers}c_{r,k}\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit k\theta/r}.  \label{p5:eq:Pr-conj-Fourier}
\end{equation}

\begin{conjecture}[Mellin-harmonic nondegeneracy]
\label{p5:conj:Mellin-nondegenerate}
Apart from coefficients forced to vanish by parity or the chosen
regularization, $c_{r,k}\ne0$ for every $k\ne0$.  Consequently the polyphase
projection
\begin{equation}
 P_1(\theta)=r\sum_{\ell\in\IntegerNumbers}c_{r,r\ell}\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit\ell\theta} \label{p5:eq:Pr-projection-conj}
\end{equation}
loses harmonics only through the exact roots-of-unity cancellation of
\cref{p5:thm:Mellin-cancellation}, not through accidental zeros of Mellin residues.
\end{conjecture}

A practical attack is to derive a rigorously convergent expression for the
regularized Mellin transform $H(s)$ and certify $H(2\pi\ImaginaryUnit k/(r\log2))\ne0$
with interval arithmetic.  The decomposition allows one to work at the longer
period $r\log2$, where individual modes may be easier to separate numerically.

\subsection{Sparse endpoint asymptotics}

The component support radius is $A_r=1/[2(1-2^{-r})]$.  General small-deviation
heuristics for geometrically weighted sums predict
\begin{equation}
 \log g_r(A_r-t)
 \sim-\frac{(\log(1/t))^2}{2r\log2},
 \qquad t\downarrow0.                                    \label{p5:eq:sparse-leading-endpoint}
\end{equation}
The full Rvachev endpoint theory in the corpus contains much finer lower
Lambert--$W$ and periodic structure.

\begin{conjecture}[Sparse Lambert--$W$ transseries]
\label{p5:conj:sparse-Lambert}
For each $r\ge1$, the endpoint $g_r(A_r-t)$ has an all-orders transseries in a
lower-branch Lambert coordinate whose algebraic coefficients are accompanied
by a nonconstant periodic function of logarithmic phase with fundamental
period $r\log2$.  Under convolution of the $r$ polyphase dilates, the periodic
coefficients combine by the Mellin projector of
\cref{p5:cor:periodic-filter} to give the ordinary period-$\log2$ Rvachev
expansion.
\end{conjecture}

This conjecture is stronger than merely replacing $\log2$ by $r\log2$ in a leading term.  The saddle location, the determinant
factor, and the phase origin must all be recomputed for $M_r$.  The reward is an
``analysis then synthesis'' derivation of the full endpoint fluctuation from
sparser components.

\subsection{Radon tomography and section algorithms}

The equality
\begin{equation}
 \cC_{w_r}p_r(2x-1)=\cR_{w_r}p_r(x-1)                      \label{p5:eq:tomography-core}
\end{equation}
invites several geometric questions.

\begin{openproblem}[Direct section computation]
Develop a certified quadrature for $F$ and $G$ based on the section integral
\eqref{p5:eq:F-section-coordinates}, compare its conditioning with direct Fourier
inversion, and determine the optimal choice of $r$ as a function of the target
point and precision.
\end{openproblem}

For $r=2$, the integrand is a product of two copies of a broader, flatter
component $g_2$.  Near the endpoints of $F$, a larger $r$ may distribute the
small-deviation event across a higher-dimensional section and improve numerical
stability; in the interior, the dimensional cost may dominate.  This tradeoff
is concrete and testable.

\begin{openproblem}[Quantile tomography]
Use
\begin{equation}
 \cC_{w_r}p_r(2G(y)-1)=\cR_{w_r}p_r(G(y)-1)=y              \label{p5:eq:quantile-tomography-problem}
\end{equation}
to construct monotone Newton or safeguarded secant schemes in which both the
residual and derivative are computed as sections of the same product density.
Investigate whether the two-level geometry yields a posteriori error bounds
stronger than generic inverse-function estimates.
\end{openproblem}

\subsection{Orthogonal polynomials and sparse spectral data}

Let $(P_n^{(r)})$ be the monic orthogonal polynomials for $g_r(x)\Differential x$.
Symmetry gives
\begin{equation}
 P_{n+1}^{(r)}(x)=xP_n^{(r)}(x)-\beta_n^{(r)}P_{n-1}^{(r)}(x). \label{p5:eq:sparse-Jacobi}
\end{equation}
The moments are explicit Bell--Bernoulli expressions, so every finite
$\beta_n^{(r)}$ is an exact rational number.

\begin{openproblem}[Endpoint-flat Jacobi asymptotics]
Determine the asymptotic expansion of $\beta_n^{(r)}$ beyond the universal
Nevai limit $A_r^2/4$.  In particular, test whether the infinitely flat,
log-periodic endpoint behavior of $g_r$ creates logarithmic or periodic
corrections to the usual algebraic recurrence-coefficient asymptotics.
\end{openproblem}

The polyphase convolution gives a second route: the Jacobi data of $u$ should
be constrained by the $r$ component moment sequences and their scaled free
cumulant-like convolution algebra.  No simple formula is expected, but exact
Hankel determinants at moderate order may reveal a stable pattern.

\subsection{Twisted scale characters}

The ordinary polyphase split uses the trivial character of the cyclic group
$\IntegerNumbers/r\IntegerNumbers$.  Near the origin, where logarithms are single-valued, introduce
\begin{equation}
 L_{r,\ell}(z)=\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\omega^{-a\ell}
 \log\Psi_r(z/2^a),
 \qquad \omega=\EulerE^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit/r}.                            \label{p5:eq:twisted-scale-log}
\end{equation}
Then $L_{r,0}=\log\Phi$, while the nontrivial $L_{r,\ell}$ isolate scale
characters which cancel in the ordinary product.

\begin{openproblem}[Scale-character continuation]
Find natural single-valued analytic or meromorphic objects whose local
logarithms are $L_{r,\ell}$, describe their zero divisors, and determine whether
they encode the discarded Mellin harmonics directly.  A successful construction
would diagonalize modular scale refinement in the same way that the finite
Fourier transform diagonalizes cyclic translation.
\end{openproblem}

Complex powers of factors cannot simply be written globally because zeros
create monodromy.  A divisor-valued or line-bundle formulation may be the right
language.

\subsection{General bases and residue subsets}

For $b>1$, the formal analogue
\begin{equation}
 \Psi_{b,r}(z)=\prod_{m\ge0}\SincPi(z/b^{rm})              \label{p5:eq:base-b-sparse}
\end{equation}
has a base-$b$ polyphase decomposition.  Much of the generic base-$b$ theory is
already present in the corpus, so the frontier is not the definition itself.
The new questions are structural:
\begin{itemize}
\item classify residue subsets $A\subset\{0,\ldots,r-1\}$ for which the
      partial product is a characteristic function with especially simple
      compact support or refinement equation;
\item determine the lobe-sign automatic sequence when $b$ is not even, where
      the parity simplification in \eqref{p5:eq:sparse-sign-base} changes;
\item replace binary signs by roots of unity and recover generalized
      Prouhet--Thue--Morse words from distributional derivatives of complex or
      vector-valued box splines;
\item identify the exact Mellin character projector for arbitrary scale
      residue weights.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Arithmetic of sparse knot limits}

All finite knot locations, cumulants, moments, and power sums are rational for
$q=2^{-r}$.  The limiting density values are subtler.

\begin{openproblem}[Sparse-adic values]
Determine the arithmetic nature of $g_r(x)$ and its derivatives at rational
points whose denominators are powers of $2^r$.  Establish either a rationality
theorem with denominator bounds, analogous to the dyadic Fabius theory, or a
sharp obstruction showing why the self-CDF mechanism at $r=1$ is exceptional.
\end{openproblem}

The plateau present for $r\ge2$ supplies many trivial rational values, but the
transition regions carry the real arithmetic content.  The nested
factorization \eqref{p5:eq:nested-gr} may transfer information between different
$r$.

\subsection{Formalization roadmap}

The new theorem chain is unusually well suited to formal verification because
most proofs reduce to finite reindexing, characteristic functions, and
polynomial distribution identities.

A practical Lean sequence is:
\begin{enumerate}
\item define $\Psi_r$ as a locally uniformly convergent entire product and
      prove the partition $n=a+rm$;
\item construct $Z_r$ from the existing weighted-uniform probability
      infrastructure and identify its characteristic function;
\item prove the finite and infinite convolution factorizations;
\item formalize zero multiplicities using $\TwoAdicValuation$ and Euclidean division;
\item define $g_{r,N}$ as a finite convolution, then prove the truncated-power
      formula and $D^Ng_{r,N}$ as a finite signed measure;
\item prove the bit-reversal ordering and parity invariance;
\item derive the power-sum identity by distributional integration by parts,
      or first in the polynomial finite-difference algebra and then transfer;
\item connect finite cumulants to Bell polynomials and Bernoulli numbers;
\item formalize the base-$2^r$ lobe-sign theorem using the valuation-counting
      identity;
\item treat Radon/coarea statements last, since they require the heaviest
      measure-geometric library support.
\end{enumerate}
The finite jump and power-sum layer can be formalized without first developing
all of infinite-product complex analysis, making it a good initial target.

\begin{frontierbox}[Highest-value next experiments]
The most decisive near-term computations are: interval certification of the
first sparse Mellin harmonics; high-order exact Jacobi coefficients for
$g_r$; uniform-error measurements for \eqref{p5:eq:strong-tail-conj}; and a direct
comparison of Fourier versus Radon-section evaluation of $F$ and $G$.  Each
experiment tests a precise structural prediction and can feed directly into a
proof attempt.
\end{frontierbox}

\section{Conclusion}
\label{p5:sec:conclusion}

The Fabius--Rvachev system is unusually representation-rich because a single
object lies at the intersection of compactly supported smooth analysis,
probability, automatic sequences, infinite products, spectral determinants,
and digital asymptotics.  The audited repository already develops most of the
classical and several frontier realizations.  The main purpose of this report
has been to add a layer that cuts across those existing themes rather than
repeat them.

The central operation is Euclidean division of the Fourier scale index.  From
that elementary reindexing follow:
\begin{enumerate}
\item a sparse characteristic function $\Psi_r$, its compact density $g_r$,
      and a unique polyphase convolution factorization of $u$;
\item staircase cosine exponents, quotient-valued zero multiplicities,
      Gamma and canonical products, a sparse spectral zeta, exact determinant
      splitting, and cumulant recombination;
\item a roots-of-unity projector on Mellin poles and logarithmic-scale
      harmonics;
\item a product-density Radon representation in which a Fabius value is both a
      half-space volume and a parallel hyperplane section;
\item finite sparse splines whose highest derivative jumps, distributional
      derivative, Fourier quotient, and cardinal integrals all recover
      Thue--Morse signs;
\item an exact left-to-right reading of the ordinary Thue--Morse word from the
      spatially ordered jumps;
\item Bell--Bernoulli formulas for every surviving signed power sum and for the
      separate evil and odious power sums;
\item an infinite product of uniform averaging operators with an exact
      Bernoulli/zeta differential logarithm;
\item a twisted Poisson family and a zero-slope sine series for $F$;
\item a factorization of binary Thue--Morse parity into overlapping
      base-$2^r$ digit-parity characters, together with recovery from odd zero
      slopes of $\Phi$.
\end{enumerate}

Several of these statements are short once the correct object is introduced.
That is a strength rather than a weakness: the polyphase factor exposes a
common mechanism behind formulas which otherwise look unrelated.  The next
stage should combine the sparse Mellin analysis, endpoint Lambert--$W$
coordinates, and the exact finite jump calculus.  Those three layers offer a
credible route from computer-assisted experimentation to theorem-level control
of periodic asymptotics and approximation errors.

\section{Selected identities in machine-friendly form}
\label{p5:app:machine-identities}

For use in symbolic systems, the main new identities can be summarized without
prose as follows.  Put $q=2^{-r}$.
\begin{align}
 \Psi_r(z)&=\prod_{m\ge0}\SincPi(q^mz),                    \label{p5:eq:machine-1}\\
 \Phi(z)&=\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}\Psi_r(2^{-a}z),                \label{p5:eq:machine-2}\\
 \log\Psi_r(z)&=-\sum_{m\ge1}\frac{\zeta(2m)}{m(1-q^{2m})}z^{2m}, \label{p5:eq:machine-3}\\
 \OrderOperator_{z=n}\Psi_r&=1+\Floor{\frac{\TwoAdicValuation(n)}r}, \label{p5:eq:machine-4}\\
 \mathcal Z_r(s)&=\frac{\zeta(s)}{1-2^{-rs}},             \label{p5:eq:machine-5}\\
 g_{r,N}(x)&=\frac{q^{-\binom N2}}{(N-1)!}
 \sum_{k<2^N}\eps_k(x-x_{r,N,k})_+^{N-1},                \label{p5:eq:machine-6}\\
 D^Ng_{r,N}&=q^{-\binom N2}\sum_{k<2^N}\eps_k\delta_{x_{r,N,k}}, \label{p5:eq:machine-7}\\
 \eps_k&=q^{\binom N2}[D^{N-1}g_{r,N}]_{x_{r,N,k}},       \label{p5:eq:machine-8}\\
 \FourierTwoPi{g_{r,N}}(z)&=\Psi_r(z)/\Psi_r(q^Nz),            \label{p5:eq:machine-9}\\
 \sum_{k<2^N}\eps_kx_{r,N,k}^{N+m}
 &=(-1)^Nq^{\binom N2}\frac{(N+m)!}{m!}\mu_m^{(r,N)},    \label{p5:eq:machine-10}\\
 \eps_n&=\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{s_{2^r}(\Floor{n/2^a})}, \label{p5:eq:machine-11}\\
 \eps_n&=-\Sign\Phi'(2n+1).                               \label{p5:eq:machine-12}
\end{align}
These formulas use the Fourier convention \eqref{p5:eq:Fourier-convention}; a
computer algebra implementation using another convention must modify every
$2\pi$ factor consistently.

\begin{abstract}
This report gives a comprehensive, snapshot-relative audit of the recursive LaTeX corpus
under \path{Analysis/FabiusFunction/docs}.  The audit covers the 67-path pinned ledger at
commit \texttt{32d6d36c51d803289e6d6a0dc0c37753766eba47}, the living primary and
frontier manuscripts, the Thue--Morse formula atlases, the Fourier-decay campaign, the
inverse-function and self-sampling drafts, the archive lineages, and the paper-source
transcriptions.  Existing results are organized into a representation atlas spanning
probability laws, infinite convolutions, finite Thue--Morse splines, sinc and cosine
products, Fourier inversion, gamma and zero products, moment/cumulant/Bell algebra,
q-binomial formulas, exact dyadic arithmetic, Lambert--\(W\) endpoint asymptotics,
Cauchy transforms, one-sided transforms, Fredholm determinants, and Jacobi continued
fractions.  Repository results, immediate recombinations, and new corpus-relative results
are explicitly distinguished.

The first-wave frontier constructions are retained: the Cauchy--Stieltjes transform of
Rvachev's up-function, its logarithmic dyadic fixed point, the functional-differential law
\[
 R'(z)=2\{R(2z+1)-R(2z-1)\},
\]
the all-orders Thue--Morse derivative tree, finite logarithmic resolvents, exact transforms
of \(F\), one-sided up, and \(F^{-1}\), the cotangent/digamma/Hurwitz-zeta hierarchy for
\(\Phi'/\Phi\), a trace-class Fredholm determinant for \(\Phi\), and the positive Jacobi
fraction of the up-law.

The second wave develops several different representation mechanisms.  Compact support
and the vanishing integer samples of \(\Phi\) give the sparse half-lattice reconstruction
\[
 \Phi(z)=\SincPi(2z)+
 \sum_{k\ge0}\Phi(k+\tfrac12)
 \bigl[\SincPi(2z-2k-1)+
       \SincPi(2z+2k+1)\bigr],
\]
and hence a convergent Mittag--Leffler expansion whose residues recover both signed and
unsigned Thue--Morse.  Fourier duality gives odd-harmonic cosine series for up and \(F\)
and the infinite weighted Prouhet identities
\[
 \sum_{k\ge0}(-1)^{\BinaryDigitSum(k)}|\Phi(k+\tfrac12)|(2k+1)^{2r}
 =\begin{cases}\tfrac12,&r=0,\\0,&r\ge1.\end{cases}
\]
A Bose--Malmsten kernel converts the sinc product into an exponential integral and yields
all logarithmic derivatives.  Mellin transforms of up and \(F\), the quantile moments of
\(F^{-1}\), and the generalized binomial series of the up-moments are unified by one
entire function.

For the signed Thue--Morse generating function \(E(z)\), a new formal orthogonality
analysis proves
\[
 p_{2n}(x)=p_n(x^2),\qquad a_n=(-1)^{n+1},
\]
and gives an exact nonlinear dyadic recursion for the remaining Jacobi coefficients.
At \(N=2^m\), \(p_N(x)=x^N+1\), producing the closed Pad\'e approximant
\[
 E(z)=\frac{\prod_{j<m}(1-z^{2^j})}{1+z^N}+O(z^{2N}).
\]
Its roots-of-minus-one partial fractions furnish an \(N\)-node complex quadrature that
reproduces \(2N\) consecutive Thue--Morse signs.  Krawtchouk, contour, residue, integral,
and antiperiodic Fourier representations of the signed and unsigned words are collected as
well.  A fully commented Python program, exact rational data, high-precision residuals,
and four reproducible figures accompany the report.  Every theorem labelled as new is
proved here; conjectures are clearly separated from established statements.
\end{abstract}


\newpage

\section{Executive summary and novelty boundary}

\subsection{What was audited}

The repository contains several kinds of LaTeX source that must not be conflated:

\begin{enumerate}
\item a living primary synthesis of the formalized and analytic theory;
\item a large consolidated non-formalized frontier volume;
\item current draft reports on arithmetic rays, half-integer spectra, self-sampling,
      inverse asymptotics, Newton/exponent-sequence hierarchies, Fourier decay, and related
      topics;
\item archived earlier lineages, retained because some contain historically distinctive
      formulas even when superseded;
\item seven paper-source transcriptions or corrected transcriptions.
\end{enumerate}

The repository's own pinned audit ledger records 67 LaTeX paths at the following commit:
\begin{center}
\small\texttt{32d6d36c51d803289e6d6a0dc0c37753766eba47}.
\end{center}
The live tree inspected on 27 August 2026 contains additional current drafts, notably the
self-sampling, inverse, and Newton/Rvachev reports.  Appendix~\ref{p6:app:corpus} records the pinned ledger and the live
additions used in this study.

\subsection{Non-duplication protocol}

Every formula in the atlas is tagged conceptually as follows.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}c p{0.78\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Tag & meaning \\
\midrule
\statusknownPartl & already explicit in the audited repository or classical source; included for orientation \\
\statusderivedPartl & an immediate transform or recombination of known identities; useful but not claimed as new \\
\statusnewPartl & apparently absent from the audited corpus; proved or conjectured here \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The word ``new'' throughout this report means \emph{new relative to the audited repository}.
No claim of global bibliographic priority is made without a separate literature search.
In particular, the current frontier report explicitly proposes a Jacobi continued fraction
for the moment generating function or Cauchy transform as an open direction.  The present
report constructs that fraction and develops its consequences.

\subsection{Main new results}

The most substantial additions are:

\begin{enumerate}
\item the Cauchy transform \(R\) and its moment, Laplace-product, Fourier--Laplace,
      boundary-value, and logarithmic fixed-point representations;
\item the exact resolvent equation and its all-orders Thue--Morse derivative tree;
\item a finite-level \(2^N\)-term logarithmic formula converging to \(R\), paired by
      boundary jumps with the finite Thue--Morse spline density;
\item the Jacobi \(J\)-fraction of \(R\), the contracted Stieltjes \(S\)-fraction of the
      even moments, exact rational coefficients through order 40, Gaussian quadrature
      convergents, and the theorem \(\beta_n\to1/4\);
\item a Fredholm determinant realization of the Fourier image and a trace-resolvent
      interpretation of its logarithmic derivative;
\item cotangent, tangent, valuation-weighted partial-fraction, digamma, and Hurwitz-zeta
      representations of the logarithmic derivatives of the Fourier image;
\item exact one-sided Laplace and Fourier transforms of \(\RvachevUp\), exact transforms of
      \(F\) on \([0,1]\), and a master pushforward identity for \(F^{-1}\).
\end{enumerate}

\section{Normalization and structural dictionary}

\subsection{The functions}

Let \(F:[0,1]\to[0,1]\) denote the bounded Fabius function \cite{p6:Fabius1966,p6:AriasTranslation}, extended by \(0\) on
\(( -\infty,0]\) and by \(1\) on \([1,\infty)\).  On the unit interval it satisfies
\begin{align}
 F(0)&=0, & F(1)&=1, & F(1-x)&=1-F(x), \label{p6:eq:F-symmetry}\\
 F'(x)&=2F(2x) &&(0\le x\le\tfrac12). \label{p6:eq:F-diff}
\end{align}
The Rvachev function is written
\begin{equation}
 \rho(x):=\RvachevUp(x)=
 \begin{cases}
 F(x+1),&-1\le x\le0,\\
 F(1-x),&0\le x\le1,\\
 0,&|x|\ge1.
 \end{cases}                                                \label{p6:eq:rho-fold}
\end{equation}
Then \(\rho\) is even, strictly positive on \((-1,1)\), normalized by
\(\int\rho=1\), flat at \(\pm1\), and satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \rho'(x)=2\bigl(\rho(2x+1)-\rho(2x-1)\bigr).                \label{p6:eq:rho-dde}
\end{equation}

\subsection{Fourier convention and basic transforms}

We use
\begin{equation}
 \FourierTwoPi{f}(\xi)=\int_{\RealNumbers}f(x)e^{-2\pi i\xi x}\,\Differential x,
 \qquad
 f(x)=\int_{\RealNumbers}\FourierTwoPi{f}(\xi)e^{2\pi i\xi x}\,\Differential\xi.       \label{p6:eq:fourier-convention}
\end{equation}
Set
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(\xi):=\FourierTwoPi{\rho}(\xi),
 \qquad
 \SincPi(z):=\frac{\sin(\pi z)}{\pi z},\quad \SincPi(0)=1. \label{p6:eq:Phi-def}
\end{equation}
The classical products are
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}\SincPi\!\left(\frac{z}{2^n}\right)
 =\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}\cos\!\left(\frac{\pi z}{2^m}\right)^m. \label{p6:eq:Phi-products}
\end{equation}
The scaling law is
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\SincPi(z)\Phi(z/2).                                \label{p6:eq:Phi-scaling}
\end{equation}

\subsection{The probability model}

Let \(U_1,U_2,\dots\) be independent random variables, uniform on \([-1,1]\), and set
\begin{equation}
 X=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{-k}U_k.                              \label{p6:eq:X-series}
\end{equation}
Then \(X\) has density \(\rho\).  Equivalently,
\begin{equation}
 X\overset{\mathrm d}=\frac{U+X'}2,                           \label{p6:eq:X-fixed-point}
\end{equation}
where \(U\sim\operatorname{Unif}[-1,1]\) and \(X'\) is an independent copy of \(X\).
The moment generating function is
\begin{equation}
 M(t):=\Expectation e^{tX}
 =\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\sinh(t/2^k)}{t/2^k}
 =\Phi\!\left(\frac{it}{2\pi}\right).                       \label{p6:eq:M-product}
\end{equation}
The even moments are
\begin{equation}
 \mu_{2n}:=\Expectation X^{2n}=\int_{-1}^{1}x^{2n}\rho(x)\,\Differential x,
 \qquad \mu_{2n+1}=0.                                      \label{p6:eq:moments}
\end{equation}
Finally, if
\begin{equation}
 Y=\frac{X+1}{2},                                             \label{p6:eq:Y-affine}
\end{equation}
then \(F\) is the distribution function of \(Y\), and
\begin{equation}
 F'(x)=2\rho(2x-1),\qquad 0<x<1.                              \label{p6:eq:F-density}
\end{equation}

\section{Audited representation atlas}

This section compresses the principal representation families already present in the
repository and places the new formulas beside them.  Proofs of the new entries begin in
Section~\ref{p6:sec:cauchy}.

\subsection[Real-space and probability representations of rho]{Real-space and probability representations of \(\rho\)}

\paragraph{Infinite convolution (\statusknownPartl).}
Since \(2^{-k}U_k\) is uniform on \([-2^{-k},2^{-k}]\),
\begin{equation}
 \rho=\mathop{\ast}_{k=1}^{\infty}
 \left(2^{k-1}\IndicatorOf{[-2^{-k},2^{-k}]}\right).        \label{p6:eq:rho-convolution}
\end{equation}
This is the real-space counterpart of the sinc product.

\paragraph{Infinite cube integral (\statusderivedPartl).}
For every bounded continuous test function \(g\),
\begin{equation}
 \int_{-1}^{1}g(x)\rho(x)\,\Differential x
 =\int_{[-1,1]^{\NaturalNumbers}}g\!\left(\sum_{k\ge1}2^{-k}u_k\right)
   \prod_{k\ge1}\frac{\Differential u_k}{2}.                           \label{p6:eq:cube-pushforward}
\end{equation}
Formally, this may be written as a Dirac-delta integral for \(\rho(x)\).

\paragraph{Finite Thue--Morse splines (\statusknownPartl).}
Put
\begin{equation}
 X_N=\sum_{k=1}^{N}2^{-k}U_k,
 \qquad
 a_{N,j}=\frac{2^N-1-2j}{2^N},
 \qquad
 \eps_j=(-1)^{\BinaryDigitSum(j)}.                               \label{p6:eq:aNj}
\end{equation}
The density \(\rho_N\) of \(X_N\) is
\begin{equation}
 \rho_N(x)=\frac{2^{N(N-1)/2}}{(N-1)!}
 \sum_{j=0}^{2^N-1}\eps_j\,(x+a_{N,j})_+^{N-1}.             \label{p6:eq:finite-spline}
\end{equation}
For each fixed derivative order, \(\rho_N\to\rho\) uniformly after \(N\) is sufficiently
large relative to that order.  The repository develops several centered and one-sided
variants with explicit error control.

\paragraph{Fourier inversion and cosine form (\statusknownPartl).}
Because \(\Phi\) is real and even,
\begin{align}
 \rho(x)&=\int_{\RealNumbers}\Phi(\xi)e^{2\pi i x\xi}\,\Differential\xi,
                                                                  \label{p6:eq:rho-fourier-inversion}\\
 \rho(x)&=2\int_0^{\infty}\Phi(\xi)\cos(2\pi x\xi)\,\Differential\xi.
                                                                  \label{p6:eq:rho-cosine}
\end{align}
The audited corpus also contains Poisson-summation formulas, Legendre expansions,
periodized cosine series, dyadic spline limits, and endpoint transseries.  They are not
rederived here.

\subsection[Product, zero, gamma, and exponential representations of Phi]{Product, zero, gamma, and exponential representations of \(\Phi\)}

\paragraph{Canonical zero product (\statusknownPartl).}
Let \(\TwoAdicValuation(n)\) be the exponent of \(2\) in \(n\).  The nonzero integer \(n\) is a zero of
multiplicity \(1+\TwoAdicValuation(|n|)\), and
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}
 \left(1-\frac{z^2}{n^2}\right)^{1+\TwoAdicValuation(n)}.                \label{p6:eq:canonical-product}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Gamma product (\statusderivedPartl).}
Euler's reflection identity gives
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}
 \frac{1}{\Gamma(1+z/2^k)\Gamma(1-z/2^k)}.                  \label{p6:eq:gamma-product}
\end{equation}
This is the natural entry point for the Barnes and multiple-gamma compressions studied in
current repository drafts.

\paragraph{Spectral-zeta exponential (\statusknownPartl).}
For \(|z|<1\),
\begin{equation}
 \log\Phi(z)
 =-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}
 \frac{\zeta(2m)}{m(1-2^{-2m})}z^{2m}.                       \label{p6:eq:logPhi-zeta}
\end{equation}
The coefficient sequence is the spectral zeta function
\begin{equation}
 Z_{\Phi}(s)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1+\TwoAdicValuation(n)}{n^s}
 =\frac{\zeta(s)}{1-2^{-s}}.                                \label{p6:eq:spectral-zeta}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Fredholm determinant (\statusnewPartl).}
Section~\ref{p6:sec:fredholm} upgrades \eqref{p6:eq:canonical-product} to
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)=\det(I-z^2\cK),                                    \label{p6:eq:fredholm-preview}
\end{equation}
for an explicit positive trace-class diagonal operator \(\cK\).  This makes
\eqref{p6:eq:spectral-zeta} literally the trace zeta function of \(\cK\).

\subsection{Moment, cumulant, and Bell-polynomial representations}

The moment generating function has the convergent Taylor series
\begin{equation}
 M(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mu_{2n}\frac{t^{2n}}{(2n)!}.       \label{p6:eq:M-moment-series}
\end{equation}
Its logarithm is
\begin{equation}
 \log M(t)=
 \sum_{m=1}^{\infty}
 \frac{2^{2m-1}B_{2m}}{m(2m)!(4^m-1)}t^{2m},                \label{p6:eq:M-cumulant}
\end{equation}
so all moments are complete Bell polynomials in explicit Bernoulli/Mersenne cumulants.
These formulas, together with reciprocal Bell expansions for \(1/\Phi\), are developed
extensively in the repository.

\subsection[Transform representations of F and inverse F]{Transform representations of \(F\) and \(F^{-1}\)}

The CDF inversion formula gives
\begin{equation}
 F(x)=\frac12+\frac1\pi\int_0^{\infty}
 \Phi(\xi)\frac{\sin(2\pi(2x-1)\xi)}{\xi}\,\Differential\xi,
 \qquad 0<x<1.                                               \label{p6:eq:F-CDF-Fourier}
\end{equation}
Section~\ref{p6:sec:fabius-transforms} adds closed Laplace and Fourier transforms of \(F\) as
a function, as well as transforms of its derivative measure and its inverse.
